Coordinated attacks during November 2020 targeted many freight trucks on the N3 and [N12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N12_\(South_Africa\)) routes.[^cite_note-en1-457] The attackers managed to loot and destroy 35 trucks[^cite_note-gnsb1-204] in [KwaZulu-Natal](193596.md) and at [Heidelberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heidelberg,_Gauteng),[^cite_note-tk1-458] [Parys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parys)-[Sasolburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasolburg)[^cite_note-gnsb1-204] and [Daveyton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daveyton)[^cite_note-ml2-459] respectively. Truck drivers were shot at, with some injured and one killed.[^cite_note-ml1-460] 25 people were arrested.[^cite_note-gnsb1-204]

[Faiez Jacobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faiez_Jacobs) pointed out that arson cripples the entire value chain of the community‚ with many people unable to go to work‚ losing a day's pay‚ and ultimately losing their employment, which in turn causes social upheaval and adds further burdens to their community.[^cite_note-nomb-461] [SATAWU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Transport_and_Allied_Workers_Union) has noted that labour strikes that are called by unidentifiable persons have been associated with incidents of arson.[^cite_note-462]

### Explosives

[Organised crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organised_crime) syndicates smuggle blasting cartridges across South Africa's borders, especially from [Zimbabwe](34399.md), and their unsuspecting mules deliver these to criminals. The explosives are used to bomb ATMs, rob cash-in-transit vehicles, for [illegal mining](49640132.md) operations, or more recently as a tool of extortion during robberies. The Explosives Act of 2003 and its supporting regulations have not been implemented as of 2021,[^cite_note-ea56-463] and law enforcement relies on the outdated Act 26 of 1956 and regulations that were only superficially updated[^cite_note-er2002-464] since 1972. These regulations do not place mandatory obligations on tracking and tracing of explosives, meaning that legal consumers are not prosecuted when their explosives are lost or stolen.[^cite_note-chel1-93]

### Illegal mining

See also: [Zama zamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zama_zamas)

[Illegal mining](49640132.md) is most prevalent in Gauteng, Mpumalanga, Limpopo, North West and the Free State.[^cite_note-dla1-465] It shows an upward trend and is inter-related with organised crime[^cite_note-mcsa1-466][^cite_note-ada2-467] and [money laundering](19390.md).[^cite_note-mm6-468] Thousands of disused or active mines attract illegal miners, also known as zama zamas, due to unanswered socio-economic inequalities. The estimated 30,000 illegal miners are organised by some 200 criminal syndicates (as of 2022[^cite_note-led1-469]) which infiltrate industrial gold mines, where they employ violent means[^cite_note-ada2-467] and exploitative working conditions.[^cite_note-iol4-470]

Losses in sales, tax revenue and royalties are said to amount to R21 billion per annum, while physical infrastructure and public safety are compromised. Output in excess of R14 billion of gold per annum has been channeled to international markets via neighbouring countries. The greater part, over 34 tons of gold between 2012 and 2016, was smuggled to [Dubai](211583.md), UAE. At times construction contractors rent out their [haul trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haul_truck) and [excavators](312903.md) to syndicates who then proceed with [open-cast mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-pit_mining) in contravention of the [Environmental Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Environmental_Management_Act,_1998).[^cite_note-cc1-471]

Mining companies which operate from no fixed address may also submit fraudulent applications for mining licences, by for instance plagiarizing the required [EIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_assessment).[^cite_note-afri2-472]

Unregistered operators of precious metal refineries are charged with illegal possession of unwrought precious metals under the Precious Metals Act, cyanide pollution under the Environmental Act, and their equipment and raw materials are seized.[^cite_note-iol3-473][^cite_note-vil1-474] The Mineral and Petroleum Resources Development Act of 2002 acknowledges artisanal miners, but an overhaul of the act has been proposed.[^cite_note-mart-475]

The [Council for Geoscience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_for_Geoscience) and [Department of Mineral Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Mineral_Resources_and_Energy) are jointly responsible for rehabilitating the 6,000 abandoned mines in South Africa (600 around Johannesburg alone).[^cite_note-bing1-476] They are barely making headway, and don't expect to close all abandoned shafts before 2039.[^cite_note-dla1-465] Meanwhile, manpower and resources of the Border Management Authority (BMA) will be diverted to combat well-armed illegal miners.[^cite_note-def2-477]

### Livestock and produce theft

Livestock theft is prevalent and increasing[^cite_note-ste2-478] in all provinces of South Africa, but the [Eastern Cape](313645.md), where gangs of thieves openly target marginal farmers,[^cite_note-dis1-479] has the highest number of cases.[^cite_note-eck-480] Some 70,000 head of cattle to the value of R1,3 billion are stolen annually, while sheep farming has been abandoned in some areas due to the prevalence of theft. Livestock theft is often motivated by greed,[^cite_note-kg1-481][^cite_note-mas4-482][^cite_note-hey2-483] and organized theft which bags a large number of animals at a time is on the rise, representing about 88% of these thefts in 2019.[^cite_note-ste1-290][^cite_note-eck-480]

The losses impact the livelihoods of farm workers besides farmers,[^cite_note-nn1-484] and it is claimed that crime prevention has yet to catch up with the *modus operandi* of syndicates.[^cite_note-eck-480] A stock theft task force has been established in the [Free State](447006.md),[^cite_note-ns3-27] and [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom) has provided a hotline for the reporting of suspicious activities by its employees, after alleged stock theft on a [Northern Cape](447008.md) farm.[^cite_note-esk1-485]

Since 1996, ARC's Animal Genetics Laboratory in [Irene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irene,_Gauteng), Pretoria, assists the SAPS in about 500 cases per annum where DNA matching may provide identification, determine parentage or resolve ownership of stolen livestock or poached game.[^cite_note-zwa1-486]

Since 2015, theft of [avocado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avocado) and [macadamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macadamia) produce has shown an upward trend which affects smallholder as well as large scale farmers. Locally avocado fetches up to R25 per fruit in 2020, while macadamia, of which South Africa is the largest producer, fetches up to \$25 per pound internationally. During the autumn harvest season, producers consequently rely on costly round the clock security measures to dissuade thieves. Single opportunists besides organised syndicates are at work, which via middle men supply a thriving black market.[^cite_note-iol5-487][^cite_note-times1-488]

Grain producers are sometimes conned by [silo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silo) operators that issue fraudulent silo certificates, which may misstate the quantity, quality and/or location where grain is stored, with resultant financial losses for the producer.[^cite_note-afri1-489]

### Housebreaking

In Statistics South Africa's 2025/26 Governance, Public Safety and Justice Survey (GPSJS), housebreaking was the most common crime experienced by households. The survey estimated more than 1.5 million incidents, affecting approximately 1.08 million households, or 5.3% of households nationwide. Among households that experienced housebreaking, 48.2% reported some or all incidents to the police, compared with 43.4% in 2024/25.[^cite_note-490]

The survey distinguishes housebreaking, which occurs while residents are away, from home robbery, which occurs while residents are present.[^cite_note-491]

### Municipal property

Vandalism and theft of municipal infrastructure have an impact on municipal budgets, interrupts service delivery, and burdens the taxpayer.[^cite_note-les-492] Gates, fencing, man-hole covers, paving stones, filling material of road embankments,[^cite_note-qam1-493] metal sheeting,[^cite_note-nxu1-494] any copper objects (which fetches R80/kg),[^cite_note-mc1-495] brass water meters,[^cite_note-van1-496] street lights, pipes, bathroom taps and parts of statues are stolen,[^cite_note-voa1-497][^cite_note-wat1-498][^cite_note-sel2-499] and smaller items are whisked away in [wheelie bins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_container) in broad daylight.[^cite_note-mos-500][^cite_note-rea-501]

Proposals to prevent these crimes include; installation of CCTV cameras, raids on scrapyards, closure of illegal scrapyards and daily updates of theft statistics.[^cite_note-les-492] In Cape Town, the specialized Copper Theft Unit (or so-called "Copperheads") was established in 2007, which arrested 275 thieves and scrap dealers during their first year of operation.[^cite_note-voa1-497] This reduced the city's annual financial loss due to [copper theft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper_theft) from R22 million to under R500,000.[^cite_note-iv1-502] Municipal land may also be sold illegally by incumbent or self-appointed council agents.[^cite_note-sap2-296][^cite_note-wa1-503] In 2010, 33 fraudulent land transactions were uncovered by the Johannesburg city council.[^cite_note-har-94] Municipal buildings are often occupied illegally, or may be rented out for personal gain.[^cite_note-kgo1-504]

### Power grid



Cut cables at [Meyerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meyerton,_Gauteng), Gauteng

A 2021 estimate placed losses due to cable theft at between R5 billion and R7 billion annually.[^cite_note-dev1-438] The [eThekwini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EThekwini_Metropolitan_Municipality), [Tshwane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Tshwane_Metropolitan_Municipality), [Ekurhuleni](3876354.md), [Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Johannesburg_Metropolitan_Municipality) and [Nelson Mandela Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela_Bay_Metropolitan_Municipality) metros in particular are pillaged by criminal syndicates and subsistence thieves, while conviction rates hover around 4%. Likewise [Telkom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telkom_\(South_Africa\)), [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom) and [Spoornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnet_Freight_Rail) each report thousands of cable theft incidents annually,[^cite_note-iv1-502] some by their own employees.[^cite_note-tim2-505]

Cable theft from train stations has endangered passengers and brought services to a standstill in parts of Gauteng and the Western Cape.[^cite_note-Ndlazi-506][^cite_note-ash1-445]

Around Johannesburg, vandalism and theft of the power grid infrastructure shows an upward trend, and the [Gauteng province](309661.md) has established a multi-disciplinary task team to curb these crimes by integrating available resources and expertise.[^cite_note-bal4-507]

Hundreds of millions of rand is lost to vandalising of street poles and theft of newly installed equipment such as supply cables and [aerial bundled cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_bundled_cable). Mini-substations, pole transformers,[^cite_note-les-492] road interchanges, and lights on pedestrian bridges are targeted by criminals and a high number of illegal connections also damage the supply transformers.[^cite_note-Mashaba-508] Cape Town experiences equipment damage and theft that impacts the electricity supply to residents, communities and road users, and results in almost constant outages in some areas.[^cite_note-quk1-509][^cite_note-quk2-510]

### Electricity theft

Illegal connections to the power grid is a common problem in major centers. As of 2020, illegal connections in [Soweto](28573.md) alone causes [Johannesburg](58893.md)'s City Power R3 million in losses daily,[^cite_note-kgm1-511] but the practice is also current in [Diepsloot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diepsloot) and [Alexandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandra,_Gauteng) where meter boxes and units are absent,[^cite_note-egw1-512] besides [Roodepoort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roodepoort), [Midrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midrand) and even the upmarket Waterfall Estate.[^cite_note-bt2-513] Not only households, but many businesses and even a church have been found to steal electricity.[^cite_note-kgm1-511][^cite_note-mm4-514]

Some [property developers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate_development) deliberately connect [smart meters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smart_meter) illegally, fail to register meters during construction, or divert power temporarily during and for the construction process.[^cite_note-bt2-513]

Johannesburg's City Power imposes a fine of R30,000 and expects the payment of all arrears before a premises is reconnected.[^cite_note-nts1-515] In addition to private premises, there are syndicates that focus on defrauding [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom).[^cite_note-san2-395][^cite_note-per2-396] By 2020, due to a culture of non-payment, [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_South_Africa) have accumulated debt of R46.1 billion (R31 billion overdue) with [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom),[^cite_note-bt2-513] and sometimes refuse to pay despite court judgements against them.[^cite_note-bn1-516] Payments meant for Eskom may also disappear in the pockets of municipal workers.[^cite_note-bl1-517]

### Fuel theft

Fuel worth over R100 million is stolen annually from [Transnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnet)'s pipelines,[^cite_note-sid1-518] and evidently sold by unlicensed retailers.[^cite_note-nge1-519] Drone technology, tactical deployments and intelligence gathering are implemented to curb the trend. Many vehicles have been seized at crime scenes, but the conviction rate of perpetrators remains close to 0%.[^cite_note-sid1-518]

The skimming of fuel from the tanks of large vehicles is another illicit practice, while at [filling stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filling_stations) up to 65% of retailers are regular victims of petrol drive-offs.[^cite_note-qam3-520] Similarly, drivers of fuel delivery vehicles are bribed to make partial deliveries,[^cite_note-hey5-521] i.a. by forging invoices, or to mix coal fuel with worthless material.[^cite_note-sta2-522] [Diesel](81761.md) theft in particular is a widespread problem which affects [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom)'s ability to operate gas turbines during peak electricity demand.[^cite_note-cru3-523]

### School plunder and vandalism

Schools are seen as easy targets for thieves looking for laptops, computers, data projectors, photocopiers,[^cite_note-son1-524] cameras and cash,[^cite_note-sow1-525][^cite_note-sim5-526] though fencing, electrical equipment, gas cylinders,[^cite_note-mla1-527] filing cabinets, desks and stationery are also stolen. Strategies to prevent burglaries include access control, upgrading of security gates, fences and burglar bars, CCTV cameras, 24-hour security and alarms linked to armed response.[^cite_note-mla1-527] Teachers have also been charged with theft and fraud at schools, and have been pressured into corrupt deals by external parties.[^cite_note-mla3-528][^cite_note-ty1-529]

Perpetrators of school vandalism are often vagrants, gangsters or local male learners, besides ex-learners and drop-outs. Implicated learners are typically experiencing learning problems or societal challenges like poverty or drug and alcohol dependency.[^cite_note-dew1-530] Local protests, whether due to lack of service delivery or other reasons, regularly result in arson or vandalism at schools.[^cite_note-SABC2-531][^cite_note-charl-532][^cite_note-maph-533]

During one week in 2018, four schools were set alight in [Mpumalanga](313647.md) province,[^cite_note-SABC-534] while over R300 million in damage was caused to 148 Gauteng and [KZN](193596.md) schools during [2021's unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_South_African_unrest).[^cite_note-nts2-535][^cite_note-isa4-536] Student protests at tertiary education facilities likewise cause millions in damage.[^cite_note-mok2-537][^cite_note-lea1-538][^cite_note-kri1-539] Criminals have also targeted teachers and school personnel.[^cite_note-ty1-529][^cite_note-ns1-540]

### Drug smuggling and consumption

South Africa has become a consumer, producer and distributor of hard drugs.[^cite_note-felix2-541] The trend is driven by [urban decay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_decay), criminal overlordship in marginalised communities,[^cite_note-glo1-542] homelessness[^cite_note-quk3-543] and police corruption,[^cite_note-nom2-544][^cite_note-bhe3-264] and is expanding into rural areas.[^cite_note-lee1-545][^cite_note-lee2-546]

[Tik](11038318.md) made its appearance in 1998, and the first laboratory was seized in the same year.[^cite_note-glo1-542] By 2005 it had surpassed other substances consumed by drug users in the [Western Cape](313646.md), including [Mandrax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methaqualone), [dagga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis) and alcohol. Production centres have shifted from South Africa to Nigeria since 2018, besides the Afghanistan–Pakistan border region, from which an [Ephedra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephedra_\(medicine\))-based substance is sourced since late 2019.[^cite_note-glo1-542] Tik addicts in townships who commit theft to sustain their habit, have been murdered in instances of mob justice.

The trade in [heroin](14034.md), ultimately obtained from [Afghanistan](737.md), has gained a foothold in cities, towns and some rural areas. The heroin trade has a corrupting effect on police, through their interactions with gangs, dealers and users.[^cite_note-haysom-547][^cite_note-mok3-548][^cite_note-hym1-549] Popular drug combinations that include heroin, are [nyaope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whoonga), sugars and unga. At least since 2011, consignments of [cocaine](7701.md) have entered South Africa through various, often smaller, ports such as [Saldanha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saldanha,_Western_Cape), [Mossel Bay](370621.md) or [Knysna](1166026.md).[^cite_note-cd2-550] It was distributed by the [28s gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numbers_gang) in the [Cape Flats](251477.md), which up to 2019 had a handler with international connections.[^cite_note-vin1-551]

Since the 1990s operatives of international drug cartels have made South Africa their home, and have carried out a slew of internecine assassinations. They are believed to launder foreign drug money, run extortion rackets, trade in illicit goods, and have succeeded in corrupting senior policemen and government officials.[^cite_note-cd1-552] Police and investigating agencies are often sidetracked by arresting smaller cogs in the underworld machine, while the kingpins manage to fly under the radar.[^cite_note-tsa3-553]

### Cigarette smuggling

South Africa, which allegedly has the biggest illegal tobacco trade in the world,[^cite_note-nga2-554][^cite_note-bou1-555] became a signatory to the [WHO protocol against tobacco smuggling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protocol_to_Eliminate_Illicit_Trade_in_Tobacco_Products) in 2013.[^cite_note-all1-556] Some 30% to 60%[^cite_note-van3-557] of cigarettes in South Africa are smuggled (compared to 10% internationally), which deprives the country of some R8[^cite_note-tha1-558] to R12 billion in annual revenue.[^cite_note-pv1-559][^cite_note-fis1-560] Tobacco warehouses are exempted from surveillance,[^cite_note-tha1-558] and in some provinces up to three quarters of stores offer cheap, illegal cigarettes for sale.[^cite_note-fis4-561]

The contraband originates in [Zimbabwe](34399.md), while [Namibia](21292.md), [Mozambique](19301.md) and [Eswatini](27451.md) serve as transit routes. Smugglers consider it a softer crime than drug trafficking, with less severe punishment for offenders. Legal consignments are regularly stolen by armed gangs,[^cite_note-fis2-562] or diverted from their intended delivery destinations.[^cite_note-mar6-563]

### Trade in protected species

South Africa is a party to [CITES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CITES), the aim of which is to ensure that international trade in specimens of wild animals and plants does not threaten their survival.[^cite_note-deff1-564] Vulnerable species in the South African context include the [bush elephant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_bush_elephant), [white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_rhinoceros) and [black rhinoceros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_rhinoceros), [ground pangolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_pangolin),[^cite_note-git1-565] [abalone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abalone), certain [whale](33777.md) species, *[Encephalartos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encephalartos)* cycads and valuable [succulent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succulent) genera such as *[Conophytum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conophytum)*.[^cite_note-ana3-566][^cite_note-bhe1-567][^cite_note-bol1-568]

In reptile laundering, animals caught in the wild are presented as captive-bred.[^cite_note-jm1-569] Trade in [poached](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poaching) reptiles affects [small adders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitis), tortoises, lizards (*[Cordylus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordylus)* and geckos) and [dwarf chameleons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradypodion), which all fetch very high prices internationally. Detection dogs have been useful in locating wildlife products in luggage, and DNA analysis is employed to determine where such products originated.[^cite_note-cp1-570]

The identification of [DNA markers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_marker) that would distinguish between captive-bred and poached reptiles is in an early stage of development.[^cite_note-jm1-569] The captive-bred lion industry continues to operate in a regulatory vacuum. The [USFWS](75289.md) has however banned the import of captive lion trophies in 2016, while a 2019 ruling made the export of lion bones, traded as "[tiger bones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_medicine)" in Asia, illegal on the grounds of [animal welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_welfare).[^cite_note-wel1-571]

### Cultural artifacts

Trade in, or vandalism of certain [cultural artifacts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_artifacts) are contraventions of the [National Heritage Resources Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Heritage_Resources_Agency), and only a fraction of conserved items are inventoried.[^cite_note-sto2-572] In 2008 government expressed concern about a rising number of thefts of artifacts and heritage objects from museums, galleries, castles and churches,[^cite_note-mem1-573] but relied on incomplete statistics, starting 1990. In the five-year period from 2012 to 2016, some 2,665 pieces from altogether 30 heritage institutions were reported lost or stolen.[^cite_note-sto2-572][^cite_note-hla1-574] Due to their age and rarity, heritage items may fetch high prices, but are completely non-renewable.[^cite_note-kin1-575]

Many military artifacts from the early 20th century have been stolen, including invaluable [ceremonial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceremonial_weapon) [daggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daggers), [swords](27863.md) and medals, which could be traded anywhere in the world.[^cite_note-mem1-573] The [Groot Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groot_Constantia) [Manor House and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iziko_Museums), an estate dating from 1685, was burgled in 2012, and thieves made off with over 20 items worth some R50 million, though 10 pieces were soon recovered. Among the porcelain items were a 300 year old vase, mid 17th-century Japanese [Arita porcelain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arita_ware) and a pair of 17th century Chinese vases.[^cite_note-xol1-576] It was reportedly the third heist by a suspected international syndicate that targeted valuable Asian porcelain pieces in museums.[^cite_note-sep1-577]

In 2016, various pieces of gold jewelry from the [Thulamela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thulamela) archeological site, on loan from [Ditsong Museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ditsong_Museums_of_South_Africa), were stolen from the museum in [Skukuza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skukuza).[^cite_note-sah1-578][^cite_note-dia1-579] Heritage experts were outraged, and expressed concerns about [Mapungubwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapungubwe) [artifacts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artifact_\(archaeology\)) displayed at the [Mapungubwe Interpretation Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapungubwe_National_Park), which are on loan from [Pretoria University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria_University).[^cite_note-sto1-580] Various institutions and museums have subsequently cut back on the simultaneous display of heritage items.

### Cross-border crime and corruption

Fifteen landward sub-units of the [SANDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Defence_Force) are deployed on the borders with [Zimbabwe](34399.md), [Eswatini](27451.md), [Mozambique](19301.md), [Lesotho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesotho), [Botswana](3464.md) and [Namibia](21292.md) to safeguard these against transnational crime, international crime syndicates and cartels, the illegal flow of undocumented migrants and illicit economic activities.[^cite_note-oli1-44] In July 2022 the [Department of Home Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_\(South_Africa\)) (DHA) launched the Border Management Authority (BMA) which aims to place border security functions of four government departments and the SAPS under one central command.[^cite_note-def1-581]

Deportations of illegal immigrants at the [Beitbridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitbridge,_South_Africa) border post were said to be a waste of money and a vicious circle due to the amount of bribery engaged in (R500 per [illegal immigrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_immigration_to_South_Africa)) by police guards and border officials.[^cite_note-sad2-582] Additional roadblocks have been effective in stopping many who bribed their way through the first checkpoints. [People smuggling](68714.md) of illegal [South Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian) immigrants is much more lucrative however, as airport officials and ground crews have conspired to form syndicates that net R60,000 per client.[^cite_note-sho1-583]

The South African [Department of Home Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Home_Affairs_\(South_Africa\)) subsequently warned South Africans not to sell their identities to illegal immigrants.[^cite_note-nai3-584] According to [A21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_A21_Campaign) and the [Trafficking in Persons Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trafficking_in_Persons_Report), South Africa has become an important source, transit as well as destination country for [human trafficking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_trafficking_in_South_Africa), with large sections of its population being vulnerable to its depredations.[^cite_note-ryk1-187][^cite_note-hos2-585]

## Organized crime

### Locally-based

Locally based [organized crime](22625.md) in [South Africa](17416221.md) has grown significantly since the late 2010s, fueled by institutional decline, a lack of [economic stability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_stability), and [corruption](3608404.md). Key activities include [human trafficking](19333662.md), smuggling, [extortion](162449.md), [arms trafficking](42210.md), and the [illicit trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illicit_trade) in goods and natural resources. Human trafficking remains a concern, with South Africa acting as both a source and destination country. Trafficking for forced labour, particularly in agriculture, and [domestic servitude](636781.md) are common, while the 2010s saw [labour exploitation](40067623.md) surpass [sex trafficking](2101268.md) in prevalence. Enforcement efforts are hindered by corruption and systemic challenges, with victims often unwilling to report abuse. Smuggling routes typically originate from neighbouring countries like [Zimbabwe](34399.md), [Mozambique](19301.md), and [Lesotho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesotho), where perpetrators use violence and bribery to traffic migrants.[^cite_note-Criminality_in_South_Africa_-_The_Organized_Crime_Index-586]

Extortion and [protection racketeering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protection_racket) have spread across industries, particularly in [Gauteng](309661.md), [Cape Town](6653.md), and [KwaZulu-Natal](193596.md), where criminal groups use violence to assert control over businesses and infrastructure projects. Kidnapping for ransom has also become increasingly organised. The illicit trade in counterfeit goods, including clothing and electronics, has expanded, with illicit tobacco products growing to over 50% of the market share by 2023, partly due to the [COVID-19](62750956.md) alcohol and tobacco bans. The rise of [cybercrime](341598.md) since 2020, including [ransomware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ransomware) and online scams, has further exacerbated the crime landscape, with South Africa becoming a hotspot for such activities. Despite existing legal frameworks, such as the [Prevention of Organised Crime Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prevention_of_Organised_Crime_Act,_1998), enforcement remains weak due to corruption, political interference, and the fragile justice system.[^cite_note-Criminality_in_South_Africa_-_The_Organized_Crime_Index-586]

### Nigerian mafia

This section is an excerpt from [Organised crime in Nigeria § South Africa](11734374.md#South_Africa).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Organised_crime_in_Nigeria\&action=edit)]

Nigerian organised crime groups established roots in South Africa in the mid-1990s following the end of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid), and operate primarily in [Johannesburg](58893.md), [Cape Town](6653.md) and [Durban](313650.md). Nigerians were initially involved in the crack cocaine trade, before moving into powder cocaine after establishing contacts in South America.[^cite_note-587] [Hillbrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillbrow) in Johannesburg was a stronghold for the Nigerian mafia for many years. After arriving in Cape Town, Nigerian syndicates operated drug and prostitution rackets in the affluent suburb of [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point) before they were forced out by the police and neighbourhood watch. They subsequently moved onto the city's northern suburbs and dominate the [Milnerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milnerton) area.[^cite_note-Organised_crime_in_Nigeria_Migration_of_the_Nigerian_mafia-588]

## Geographic Breakdown of Crime

Crime varies widely in South Africa based on frequency, type of crime, and where most crime occurs. Between 2010 and 2019, crime statistics suggest an incremental decrease in overall crime rate, though it varies greatly by classification and geographical location.[^cite_note-589]

### Major Cities

The vast majority of crime in South Africa occurs in its four largest provinces: [Gauteng](309661.md), [Western Cape](313646.md), [Eastern Cape](313645.md), and [KwaZulu-Natal](193596.md); specifically, in and approximate to the [Cape Town](6653.md), Johannesburg, and Pretoria metropolitan areas. [Murders](20171.md) in the provinces account for 83% of all murders nationally.[^cite_note-590] As per the United States' governments' South Africa [Travel Advisory](29372620.md), "Violent crime is more common in the downtown areas of big cities, especially after dark."[^cite_note-591] Since 2021, the Murder rate in South African cities has been growing steadily. *The State of Urban Safety in South Africa Report 2024* states that in 2024, at the end of an 18-year period, the murder rate in Cape Town had risen by 20%, in [Msunduzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Msunduzi_Local_Municipality) by 46%, and in [Nelson Mandela Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela_Bay_Metropolitan_Municipality) by 67%[^cite_note-592]

In the 2026 *Crime Index* ranking, the five worst cities for crime in South Africa, with their respective global ranks on the list, were [Pietermaritzburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietermaritzburg) (1), [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria) (2), [Johannesburg](58893.md) (5), [Durban](313650.md) (6), and [Gqeberha](2013184.md) (8).[^cite_note-Crime_Index_by_City_2026-18]

While some major cities experience crime throughout their suburbs, crime in others is mainly isolated to specific neighborhoods. [Johannesburg](58893.md), for example, experiences a range of crimes across the metropolitan area, with crime driven by many different factors, including [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty).[^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-16]

In contrast, [Cape Town](6653.md)'s crime occurs mainly in a small list of specific suburbs, and is driven predominantly by [gangsterism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangsterism). Thus, crime rates in South Africa's cities may be skewed significantly by the type, and location, of those crimes. Furthermore, for the average resident and visitor, the experience in terms of safety when traveling around a major city in South Africa is likely to vary greatly between cities.[^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-16]

To combat rises in crime rate, South Africa's urban areas have exploded the nation's security industry, with South Africa having over 2.7 million registered private [security guards](19283898.md), which compares to just 150,000 public police officers for the entire nation.[^cite_note-ps-593] Since 2014, the number of private security business has grown by 43% and the number of private guards, by 44%.[^cite_note-ps-593] Services by private security companies includes ICCTV surveillance, urgent armed response to client security alerts, physical patrolling of neighborhoods, etc.[^cite_note-594][^cite_note-ps-593]

### Townships

Most [South African townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Township_\(South_Africa\)) (informal settlements) are considered part of an urban area.[^cite_note-595]

Townships were created in the 1950s and 1960s by the [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid) government and are periphery communities to larger, historically White-only urban areas. They were created to house exclusively populations of colour --- Black African, Indian, and Coloured citizens.[^cite_note-ss-596] While older, more established townships might have some middle-income enclaves, the majority of township populations live in [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty). In [Khayelitsha](2766632.md), the largest township in Western Cape province, with over 400,000 residents, around 74% of households make only about R2300 (US\$200) per month and the unemployment rate is 54%.[^cite_note-597][^cite_note-ss-596] There are no longer explicit legal restrictions on who can live in townships, even though, today, over 95% of township populations are not White.[^cite_note-ss-596]

[Gangs in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangs_in_South_Africa) are most commonly found in the country's townships. In Cape Town alone, police estimate between 90 and 130 gangs, as well as over 100,000 gang members.[^cite_note-598]

Gathering data on the proportion of South African crime as occurring in townships is difficult, however the townships are often considered to be the epicenters of violent and organized crime in the nation.[^cite_note-hs-599] Critics of the South African government often attribute South Africa's apartheid history to the dangerous conditions in many township areas. Commonly cited factors that contribute to the high crime rates are long-term unemployment (60% of the national unemployment rate resides in townships), disorganized and under-resourced policing, and widespread extreme poverty.[^cite_note-hs-599][^cite_note-600]

### Rural Areas

[Rural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_area) areas of South Africa experience, along with typical crimes, certain distinct crimes that have a profound impact on its citizens. [Farm attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farm_attacks_in_South_Africa), which are known often to divulge into other severe crimes, such as robbery, [assault](1466.md), or murder. In 2022, there were over 300 farm attacks in South Africa, ending in the murders of over 50 farmers.[^cite_note-601]

The metrics are lesser than other crimes in the nation, though it has rural economic and community implications. [Livestock](25160767.md) theft resulting from farm attacks have consequences on the livelihoods of farmers and the efficiency of local farms. Livestock theft made up 1.38% of overall crimes in South Africa. Therefore, while urban crime is more prevalent, some argue that crimes like farm attacks and livestock theft are more consequential on the survival of farming businesses and their communities, because they have more immediate impact.[^cite_note-602]

## Effects

### Perceptions of safety

According to Statistics South Africa's 2025/26 Governance, Public Safety and Justice Survey, 80.7% of individuals aged 16 and older felt safe walking alone in their neighbourhood during the day, compared with 37.9% after dark. The proportion who reported taking measures to protect themselves against crime increased from 43.3% in 2024/25 to 46.6% in 2025/26.[^cite_note-603]

### Gated communities



A [neighbourhood watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighbourhood_watch) sign on the [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula)

[Gated communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gated_communities) are popular with the South African [middle-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle-class); Black as well as White.[^cite_note-econ-scp-162] Gated communities are usually protected by high perimeter walls topped with electric fencing, guard dogs, barred doors and windows and alarm systems linked to private security forces.[^cite_note-econ-scp-162]

The Gauteng Rationalisation of Local Government Affairs Act 10 of 1998, allows communities to "restrict" access to public roads in existing suburbs, under the supervision of the municipalities. The law requires that entry control measures within these communities should not deny anyone access. The Tshwane municipality failed to process many of the applications it has received, leaving many suburbs exposed to high levels of crime. Several communities successfully sued, won and are now legally restricting access.[^cite_note-604][^cite_note-605][^cite_note-606]

These measures are generally considered effective in reducing local crime.[^cite_note-607] Consequently, the number of enclosed neighbourhoods (i.e. existing neighbourhoods with controlled access to existing roads)[^cite_note-dfgatcom-608] in Gauteng has continued to grow.[^cite_note-gat-com-609]

### Private security companies

Main article: [Private security industry in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_security_industry_in_South_Africa)

The [SAPS](1228194.md) is responsible for managing 1,123 police stations across South Africa,[^cite_note-610] and as of 2020 had 187,000 service members (down from 192,000 in 2019).[^cite_note-611] By contrast South Africa had 450,000 private security guards in 2020 (and 470,000 in 2021),[^cite_note-bat1-612] besides 1.5 million qualified "but inactive" private security personnel.[^cite_note-mt2-48]

The SAPS has a strained relationship with [private security companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_security_companies), and warned them in 2021 that they must adhere to the law.[^cite_note-bat1-612] Vetting of security guards was highlighted in 2021 when 182 guards appointed in 2018 by the city of Johannesburg were found to have former convictions for murder, rape, assault or theft.[^cite_note-sip1-613]

To protect themselves and their assets, many businesses and middle-to-high-income households make use of privately owned [security companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Security_companies) with armed [security guards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_guards). The SAPS employ private security companies to patrol and safeguard certain police stations, thereby freeing fully trained police officers to perform their core function of preventing and fighting crime.[^cite_note-614] A December 2008 BBC documentary, *[Law and Disorder in Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_and_Disorder_in_Johannesburg)*, examined such firms in the Johannesburg area, including the Bad Boyz security company.

It is argued that the police response is generally too slow and unreliable in South Africa, thus private security companies offer a more efficient form of protection. Private security firms promise response times of two to three minutes.[^cite_note-615] Many levels of protection are offered, from suburban foot patrols to complete [security checkpoints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilian_checkpoint) at entry points to homes.[^cite_note-secondbestblog-194]

## Reactions

The government has been criticised for doing too little to stop crime. Provincial legislators have stated that a lack of sufficient equipment has resulted in an ineffective and demoralised [SAPS](1228194.md).[^cite_note-616] The Government was subject to particular criticism at the time of the [Minister of Safety and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Police_\(South_Africa\)) visit to [Burundi](21490998.md), for the purpose of promoting peace and democracy, at a time of heightened crime in [Gauteng](309661.md). This spate included the murder of a significant number of people, including members of the SAPS, killed while on duty.[^cite_note-617]

The criticism was followed by a ministerial announcement that the government would focus its efforts on mitigating the causes for the increase in crime by 30 December 2006. In one province alone, 19 police officers lost their lives in the first seven months of 2006.

In 2004, the government had a widely publicised gun amnesty program to reduce the number of weapons in private hands, resulting in 80,000 firearms being handed over.[^cite_note-618] In 1996 or 1997, the government has tried and failed to adopt the National Crime Prevention Strategy, which aimed to prevent crime through reinforcing community structures and assisting individuals to get back into work.[^cite_note-619]

A former Minister of Safety and Security, [Charles Nqakula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Nqakula), evoked public outcry in June 2006 when he responded to opposition MPs in [parliament](376182.md) who were not satisfied that enough was being done to counter crime, suggesting that MPs who complain about the country's [crime rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_rate) should stop complaining and [leave the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emigration).[^cite_note-620]

In November 2020, in response to the SAPS's poor logistics management, [parliament](376182.md)'s chairwoman for the portfolio committee on police, [Tina Joemat-Pettersson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tina_Joemat-Pettersson), expressed the opinion that the country was only reacting to crime, without being proactive.[^cite_note-lp1-43] Most emigrants from South Africa state that crime was a big factor in their decision to leave.[^cite_note-621][^cite_note-622]
